The date passed with no change, before Twitter announced that the removal date for checkmarks from non-paying accounts would be April 20, with the only way to acquire a blue checkmark through the paid subscription.[\[372\]](#cite_note-372) Around late April, the remaining "legacy" badges were removed, and only those subscribed to Twitter Blue remained.[\[373\]](#cite_note-373) Several notable users, however, reported having the blue check mark without having paid for it nor wanting it, such as writer [Stephen King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_King "Stephen King") and actor [Jason Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Alexander "Jason Alexander").[\[374\]](#cite_note-374)

The [Twitter Files](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_Files "Twitter Files") are a series of internal documents from [Twitter, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter,_Inc. "Twitter, Inc.") released between December 2022 and March 2023. After acquiring [Twitter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter "Twitter") on October 28, 2022, CEO Elon Musk provided these documents to journalists [Matt Taibbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Taibbi "Matt Taibbi"), [Bari Weiss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bari_Weiss "Bari Weiss"), [Lee Fang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Fang "Lee Fang"), and authors [Michael Shellenberger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Shellenberger "Michael Shellenberger"), David Zweig, and [Alex Berenson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Berenson "Alex Berenson"). Taibbi and Weiss, in coordination with Musk, published the details of the files through a series of Twitter threads.[\[375\]](#cite_note-Grynbaum-375)[\[376\]](#cite_note-Axios-2022-376)[\[377\]](#cite_note-377)[\[378\]](#cite_note-Musk-releases-2022b-378) After the initial release, some technology and media journalists observed that the documents mainly showed Twitter's policy team handling difficult decisions effectively. In contrast, some conservatives argued the documents revealed a liberal bias within Twitter.[\[379\]](#cite_note-Fischer-2022-379)[\[380\]](#cite_note-alJazeeraDec7-380)

In August 2023, a community note falsely accusing [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish "Jewish") lynching victim [Leo Frank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Frank "Leo Frank") of raping and murdering a 13-year-old girl repeatedly appeared on tweets by the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League "Anti-Defamation League") (ADL) and ADL CEO [Jonathan Greenblatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Greenblatt "Jonathan Greenblatt"). Neo-Nazi [trolls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_troll "Internet troll") created the note and cited [white supremacist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_supremacist "White supremacist") websites purporting to substantiate the note's claims. _[The Times of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_Israel "The Times of Israel")_ characterized the note's appearance as part of a pattern of Twitter's features rewarding antisemites.[\[382\]](#cite_note-382) Later that month, Greenblatt and Twitter CEO Linda Yaccarino had a dialogue about addressing hate speech on the platform. Both parties tweeted about it, sparking a backlash from many right-wing users, who accused the ADL of censorship and bias and launched a "#BanTheADL" hashtag campaign, which trended on the site. In the wake of this, Twitter users reported seeing a tweet promoted by Twitter's ad platform containing the white supremacist slogan known as the "[fourteen words](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourteen_words "Fourteen words")".[\[383\]](#cite_note-383)[\[384\]](#cite_note-384) Days later, Musk threatened to sue the ADL, blaming the organization for billions of dollars in lost revenue.[\[385\]](#cite_note-Loss-385)

In November 2023, Musk replied to an antisemitic tweet which supported the [white genocide conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_genocide_conspiracy_theory "White genocide conspiracy theory") and accused Jews of "pushing the exact kind of dialectical hatred against whites that they claim to want people to stop using against them". Musk responded that the tweet "said the actual truth".[\[387\]](#cite_note-Truth-387) Following this reply, [Musk affirmed white nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Views_of_Elon_Musk#Race_and_white_nationalism "Views of Elon Musk"), saying it was "super messed-up" that white people are not "allowed to be proud of their race".[\[388\]](#cite_note-Proud-388) Two days later, after the ADL had condemned Musk's tweet describing it as "indisputably dangerous",[\[389\]](#cite_note-389) the [White House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House "White House") issued a statement with [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") condemning Musk, proclaiming "we condemn this abhorrent promotion of antisemitic and racist hate in the strongest terms".[\[390\]](#cite_note-Condemnation-390)

> If somebody is going to try to blackmail me with advertising, blackmail me with money, go fuck yourself. Go fuck yourself. Is that clear? I hope it is

— Elon Musk, November 29, 2023[\[391\]](#cite_note-Fuck-391)

At the DealBook Summit later that month, journalist [Andrew Ross Sorkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Ross_Sorkin "Andrew Ross Sorkin") questioned Musk about the withdrawal of advertisers from X following his recent posts. Musk said in response, "I hope they stop. Don't advertise", and told advertisers to "go fuck yourself".[\[391\]](#cite_note-Fuck-391) Musk mentioned [Bob Iger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Iger "Bob Iger"), the CEO of [the Walt Disney Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Walt_Disney_Company "The Walt Disney Company"), which had stopped advertising on X; Musk also said that if X fails, it would be "because of an advertiser boycott. And that will be what bankrupts the company."[\[392\]](#cite_note-392) He later called for Iger to be fired from Disney.[\[393\]](#cite_note-393)[\[394\]](#cite_note-394)[\[395\]](#cite_note-395)

### Center for Countering Digital Hate

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Twitter_under_Elon_Musk&action=edit&section=37 "Edit section: Center for Countering Digital Hate")\]

> Sometimes it is unclear what is driving a litigation, and only by reading between the lines of a complaint can one attempt to surmise a plaintiff's true purpose, \[...\] Other times, a complaint is so unabashedly and vociferously about one thing that there can be no mistaking that purpose. This case represents the latter circumstance. This case is about punishing the defendants for their speech.

In July 2023, at the suggestion of Yaccarino,[\[397\]](#cite_note-397) X Corp sued the [Center for Countering Digital Hate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Countering_Digital_Hate "Center for Countering Digital Hate") (CCDH) over their research that suggested Twitter fails to act on 99% of hate content from [Twitter Blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_Blue "Twitter Blue") subscribers, accusing the group of "inflammatory, outrageous, and false or misleading assertions about Twitter".[\[398\]](#cite_note-398)[\[399\]](#cite_note-399)[\[400\]](#cite_note-400) The online watchdog said the lawsuit was "riddled with legal deficiencies" and in November 2023 filed for dismissal.[\[401\]](#cite_note-401) The nonprofit group also argued it was an intimidation attempt, as well as an attempt to stifle their [First Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Amendment_to_the_United_States_Constitution "First Amendment to the United States Constitution") [protected speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_speech "Protected speech").[\[402\]](#cite_note-402) According to a Coalition for Independent Technology Research survey carried out for _Reuters_, the majority of researchers surveyed fear they would be sued for studying the platform since the CCDH lawsuit.[\[202\]](#cite_note-DangResearch-202)[\[403\]](#cite_note-403) On March 25, 2024, Judge [Charles Breyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Breyer "Charles Breyer") dismissed the lawsuit in accordance with [anti-SLAPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-SLAPP "Anti-SLAPP") laws, having described parts of the case as the "most vapid extensions of law that I've ever heard". The decision was welcomed by the CEO of the CCDH, [Imran Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imran_Ahmed_\(strategist\) "Imran Ahmed (strategist)").[\[396\]](#cite_note-Robins-Early-2024-396)

On November 16, 2023, [Media Matters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_Matters "Media Matters") published analysis indicating Twitter was placing major client advertisements next to user posts containing antisemitic content, including praise for [Adolf Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Hitler "Adolf Hitler") and [Nazis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazis "Nazis"). Several prominent companies subsequently suspended their advertising on the platform, including; [IBM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM "IBM"),[\[404\]](#cite_note-404)[\[405\]](#cite_note-405) [Apple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc. "Apple Inc."),[\[406\]](#cite_note-406) [Lionsgate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lionsgate_Studios "Lionsgate Studios"),[\[407\]](#cite_note-407) [Disney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney "Disney"),[\[408\]](#cite_note-408) [Paramount](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Global "Paramount Global"), [Comcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast "Comcast"),[\[409\]](#cite_note-409) [Warner Bros. Discovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros._Discovery "Warner Bros. Discovery"),[\[410\]](#cite_note-410) [Sony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony "Sony"),[\[411\]](#cite_note-411) [Walmart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walmart "Walmart"),[\[412\]](#cite_note-412) [Coca-Cola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola "Coca-Cola"), [Uber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uber "Uber"), [Airbnb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airbnb "Airbnb"), [Ubisoft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ubisoft "Ubisoft"), [Expedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expedia "Expedia"), [Fox Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Sports "Fox Sports"), and [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix "Netflix").[\[413\]](#cite_note-413) _[The New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times "The New York Times")_ estimated the potential revenue loss to be up to $75 million.[\[414\]](#cite_note-414)

Musk announced that X Corp would file "a thermonuclear lawsuit" against Media Matters and affiliated parties,[\[416\]](#cite_note-416)[\[417\]](#cite_note-417) and called for the journalists to be jailed.[\[418\]](#cite_note-418) On November 20, 2023, X Corp filed a complaint naming Media Matters as defendant.[\[419\]](#cite_note-Lawsuit-419) Texas Attorney General [Ken Paxton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ken_Paxton "Ken Paxton") announced an investigation into Media Matters for potential fraud, referring to it as a "radical anti-free speech organization".[\[420\]](#cite_note-420)[\[421\]](#cite_note-421)[\[422\]](#cite_note-422)

> Although tough talk is not foreign to the law enforcement arena, such overt political messaging is atypical. A reasonable factfinder is likely to interpret Defendants' words as targeting Media Matters not for legitimate law enforcement purposes but instead for its protected First Amendment activities.

— [Judge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._District_Judge "U.S. District Judge") Amit P. Mehta, August 26, 2024[\[423\]](#cite_note-Johnson2024-423)

On June 13, 2024, a U.S. District judge ordered that X Corp's lawsuit against Media Matters will proceed to trial on April 7, 2025.[\[424\]](#cite_note-424) The judge, [Reed O'Connor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reed_O'Connor "Reed O'Connor"), owns [Tesla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla,_Inc. "Tesla, Inc.") stock and chose not to recuse.[\[425\]](#cite_note-Allyn-2024-425) In August, he ruled against Media Matter's request that the case be dismissed.[\[425\]](#cite_note-Allyn-2024-425) Legal experts have criticized Musk's lawsuit, deeming it "frivolous" or "bogus", and saying that it contradicts the First Amendment.[\[426\]](#cite_note-426)[\[427\]](#cite_note-427) In December 2023, Missouri Attorney General [Andrew Bailey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Bailey_\(politician\) "Andrew Bailey (politician)") opened a similar investigation into Media Matters.[\[428\]](#cite_note-428) In August 2024, a federal judge granted an injunction to halt the Missouri investigation, saying the suit was using law enforcement machinery for political ends against Media Matters.[\[423\]](#cite_note-Johnson2024-423)

In May 2025, the FTC issued a [civil investigative demand (CID)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_investigative_demand "Civil investigative demand") letter to Media Matters, seeking information on its contacts with a World Federation of Advertisers and others regarding the dropping of ads after the Media Matters report. In August 2025, a federal judge halted the FTC's investigation into Media Matters saying that Media Matters had “engaged in quintessential First Amendment activity" when it published the report on placement of ads near antisemitic material and that “It should alarm all Americans when the Government retaliates against individuals or organizations for engaging in constitutionally protected public debate. And that alarm should ring even louder when the Government retaliates against those engaged in newsgathering and reporting.” He also found that Media Matters was likely to show that the FTC had acted with "retaliatory animus".[\[429\]](#cite_note-429) However, because of the cost of defending itself, Media Matters laid off personnel.[\[430\]](#cite_note-430)

In April 2026, the FTC dropped its investigation into Media Matters and in May Media Matters withdrew its suit against the FTC.[\[431\]](#cite_note-431)[\[432\]](#cite_note-432)

On August 6, 2024, X filed an [anti-trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Competition_law "Competition law") lawsuit in the [Northern District of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Northern_District_of_Texas "United States District Court for the Northern District of Texas") against the [World Federation of Advertisers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Federation_of_Advertisers "World Federation of Advertisers"), [Unilever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unilever "Unilever"), [Mars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mars_Inc. "Mars Inc."), [CVS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CVS_Health "CVS Health") and [Ørsted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ørsted_\(company\) "Ørsted (company)"), alleging that the advertisers had conspired via their participation in the Global Alliance for Responsible Media (GARM) to withhold "billions of dollars in advertising revenue" from X.[\[433\]](#cite_note-Scarcella-433) The World Federation of Advertisers created GARM in 2019, with the group shutting down days after the lawsuit was initiated.[\[434\]](#cite_note-434) In February 2025, the Texas lawsuit was broadened to include [Lego](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lego "Lego"), [Nestlé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nestlé "Nestlé"), [Tyson Foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyson_Foods "Tyson Foods"), [Abbott Laboratories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbott_Laboratories "Abbott Laboratories"), [Colgate-Palmolive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colgate-Palmolive "Colgate-Palmolive"), [Pinterest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinterest "Pinterest") and [Shell International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shell_International "Shell International"), as advertisers previously part of the discontinued GARM group.[\[435\]](#cite_note-435) In May, some of the advertisers requested the case be dismissed stating that "antitrust law protects competition; it does not protect X from competition".[\[436\]](#cite_note-436) In March 2026, X's lawsuit was dismissed with prejudice.[\[437\]](#cite_note-437)

Commentators have described Twitter under Musk's ownership as a "free speech [free-for-all](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/free-for-all "wiktionary:free-for-all")",[\[438\]](#cite_note-Burgess-2022-438) "free-for-all [hellscape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellscape "Hellscape")",[\[439\]](#cite_note-439) and as a [right-wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-wing_politics "Right-wing politics") social network.[\[440\]](#cite_note-440)[\[441\]](#cite_note-441) The platform garnered favorable attention from [conservatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatives "Conservatives") and [Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_\(United_States\) "Republican Party (United States)") in the United States.[\[442\]](#cite_note-442)

In November 2022, author [Stephen King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_King "Stephen King"), U.S. representative [Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria_Ocasio-Cortez "Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez"), and U.S. senator [Ed Markey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Markey "Ed Markey") criticized Musk's decision to charge Twitter users for the blue checkmark.[\[445\]](#cite_note-King_2022-445)[\[446\]](#cite_note-446)[\[444\]](#cite_note-Roth112022-444) President Biden also expressed concern with Musk's plans for Twitter, saying on November 5 that it "spews lies all across the world".[\[447\]](#cite_note-447) After the layoffs, employees flooded the anonymous forum service [Blind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blind_\(app\) "Blind (app)") with negative comments about Musk,[\[448\]](#cite_note-448) with Twitter's co-founder and former CEO [Jack Dorsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Dorsey "Jack Dorsey") expressing gratitude toward laid-off employees and apologizing for growing the company too rapidly.[\[449\]](#cite_note-449) The FTC commented that it was closely monitoring developments at Twitter, stressing that Musk must abide by its [consent decrees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consent_decrees "Consent decrees"),[\[450\]](#cite_note-450) while Irish [Data Protection Commissioner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_Protection_Commissioner "Data Protection Commissioner") Helen Dixon stated that her office had reached out to Twitter to discuss privacy concerns.[\[451\]](#cite_note-451) Social media platform [Tumblr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tumblr "Tumblr") mocked the revamped verification program by allowing its users to purchase several checkmarks for their profiles.[\[452\]](#cite_note-452)[\[453\]](#cite_note-453)

On November 9, 2022, Biden expressed support for a U.S. government review of the foreign investors backing Musk's purchase, alluding to national security concerns.[\[454\]](#cite_note-BidenReview-454) However, [U.S. Treasury Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Treasury_Secretary "U.S. Treasury Secretary") [Janet Yellen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janet_Yellen "Janet Yellen") stated that she did not see a reason to investigate the acquisition and was unaware of any national security concerns.[\[455\]](#cite_note-455) Seven Democratic senators urged the FTC to investigate Musk's rapid changes to Twitter,[\[456\]](#cite_note-456) while pharmaceutical company [Eli Lilly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_Lilly_and_Company "Eli Lilly and Company") suspended all advertising campaigns on Twitter after a false tweet from an impersonator account went viral.[\[457\]](#cite_note-457) Former head of consumer product [Jeff Seibert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Seibert "Jeff Seibert") expressed disappointment and frustration over Musk's changes to Twitter.[\[93\]](#cite_note-ResignationsNYT-93) In the wake of mass employee resignations on November 17, many Twitter users posted humorous messages on the platform expressing grief and anticipating a possible shutdown of Twitter,[\[458\]](#cite_note-458)[\[459\]](#cite_note-459) with some posting links to their other social media accounts.[\[94\]](#cite_note-OfficesClosedGuardian-94)

Musk's suspension of journalists covering the [ElonJet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ElonJet "ElonJet") incident was widely condemned. [CNN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN "CNN") and _[The Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Post "The Washington Post")_, whose reporters were banned, criticized Musk's hypocrisy and impulsiveness, while [Digital Content Next](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_Content_Next "Digital Content Next") CEO Jason Kint demanded Musk explain his actions.[\[463\]](#cite_note-463) Democrats [Lori Trahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lori_Trahan "Lori Trahan"), [Yvette Clarke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yvette_Clarke "Yvette Clarke"), [Ro Khanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ro_Khanna "Ro Khanna"), [Ritchie Torres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ritchie_Torres "Ritchie Torres"), and [Martin Heinrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Heinrich "Martin Heinrich") all criticized Musk, while Democrat [Don Beyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Beyer "Don Beyer") also voiced disapproval with Musk's labeling of Mastodon links as malicious.[\[461\]](#cite_note-Terkel-2022-461) Lawmakers from the EU, France, and Germany sided with the journalists and threatened to take retaliatory action against Musk.[\[464\]](#cite_note-464)

The first weeks of Musk's tenure at Twitter were widely described as chaotic and tumultuous by the media.[\[465\]](#cite_note-Chaotic-465) Harvard professor [Sandra Sucher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandra_Sucher "Sandra Sucher") called Musk's mass layoffs "poorly handled".[\[79\]](#cite_note-LayoffsNYT2-79) Gerald Hathaway of the [Faegre Drinker Biddle & Reath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faegre_Drinker_Biddle_&_Reath "Faegre Drinker Biddle & Reath") law firm argued the opposite, believing that Musk had done what was necessary to curb Twitter's losses, assuming his claims about Twitter's losses were true.[\[466\]](#cite_note-466) Jason Wilson of the [Southern Poverty Law Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Poverty_Law_Center "Southern Poverty Law Center") criticized Musk's perceived disinterest in "policing hate speech", observing an increase in verified white nationalists and other far-right extremists.[\[467\]](#cite_note-467) [Branko Marcetic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branko_Marcetic?action=edit&redlink=1 "Branko Marcetic (page does not exist)") of socialist magazine _[Jacobin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobin_\(magazine\) "Jacobin (magazine)")_ accused Twitter of bias after several left-wing accounts were suspended.[\[468\]](#cite_note-468) The [Brookings Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookings_Institution "Brookings Institution") said that the importance of Twitter "as a platform for political discourse in the U.S." raised implications for national security,[\[454\]](#cite_note-BidenReview-454) while cybersecurity expert [Peter W. Singer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_W._Singer "Peter W. Singer") detailed multiple cybersecurity concerns stemming from Musk's acquisition.[\[469\]](#cite_note-469)

Soon after Twitter introduced viewing Tweet limits and blocked unregistered users from viewing Tweets, numerous people have voiced concerns over the decline of functionality. Mike Proulx of [Forrester Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forrester_Research "Forrester Research") expressed on a _[Reuters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuters "Reuters")_ article that the limits were "'remarkably bad' for users and advertisers already shaken by the 'chaos' Musk has brought to the platform" while Jasmine Enberg of Insider Intelligence stated her concern in the same _Reuters_ article that the move "certainly isn't going to make it any easier to convince advertisers to return."[\[470\]](#cite_note-470) In May 2023, [Ron DeSantis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_DeSantis "Ron DeSantis") launched his [2024 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_DeSantis_2024_presidential_campaign "Ron DeSantis 2024 presidential campaign") via [Twitter Spaces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Twitter_features#Spaces "List of Twitter features"), described as a good sign by entrepreneur [David Sacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_O._Sacks "David O. Sacks"), due to high interest.[\[438\]](#cite_note-Burgess-2022-438)[\[15\]](#cite_note-Ortutay-2023-15)[\[471\]](#cite_note-471)

Japanese media reported that the limiting of viewing Tweets prompted many users in the country to abandon the platform entirely, with many flocking to [Instagram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instagram "Instagram"), [Threads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Threads_\(social_network\) "Threads (social network)"), Bakusai, or [Misskey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misskey "Misskey").io.[\[472\]](#cite_note-472)[\[473\]](#cite_note-473)[\[474\]](#cite_note-474) On July 3, Japanese game publisher Yostar announced that they would cease connecting their games' accounts to Twitter, citing the API change that had occurred a few days prior as the reason.[\[475\]](#cite_note-475) The move has also lead to concerns over disaster relief efforts in the country, as many prefectures and cities use Twitter to share public information; with [Kumamoto Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kumamoto_Prefecture "Kumamoto Prefecture")'s official disaster prevention Twitter account announcing that their accounts will be indefinitely out of service on July 5.[\[476\]](#cite_note-476)[\[477\]](#cite_note-477) The viewing Tweet limit also prompted the creation of a Japanese alternate to Twitter named Taittsuu ([Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_language "Japanese language"): たいっつー), of which 100,000 users flocked to the service within the first week of inauguration. The tweet announcing the website's inauguration stated that their intended concept was to "not remove functions of paid APIs without warning".[\[478\]](#cite_note-478)[\[479\]](#cite_note-479)[\[480\]](#cite_note-480)

In September 2024, the book _[Character Limit: How Elon Musk Destroyed Twitter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Character_Limit:_How_Elon_Musk_Destroyed_Twitter "Character Limit: How Elon Musk Destroyed Twitter")_ was released. The book, by _New York Times_ reporters [Kate Conger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Conger "Kate Conger") and [Ryan Mac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Mac "Ryan Mac"), examines Musk's purchase of Twitter and its evolution under him.[\[481\]](#cite_note-481)

According to a May 2023 _Pew Research_ survey, a majority of American users say they have taken a break from Twitter in 2023, and a quarter said they were "not very or not at all likely" to continue using the platform.[\[482\]](#cite_note-482)[\[483\]](#cite_note-CountsNakanoSurge-483) Analysis conducted by research firm Sensor Tower in October 2023 found that global active daily users of X via mobile apps had steadily declined during the year after Musk acquired the company, down 16% by September 2023, while the metric showed positive growth for five other major social media platforms.[\[484\]](#cite_note-484) In November 2023, it was reported X has lost 3 million monthly [UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom "United Kingdom") visitors, down from 26.8 million since Musk's takeover, according to [Ofcom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ofcom "Ofcom").[\[485\]](#cite_note-485)

Twitter revenue halved in three years, falling from a $5 billion peak in 2021, just before the acquisition, to $2.5 billion in 2024. These losses primarily came from the departure of advertisers, with the advertising sector falling by $2.8 billion.[\[486\]](#cite_note-486)
